<div id="mwAQ" class="section" mw-section-id="0">

<div id="mwAg"
class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux"

Curve used in computer graphics and related fields

</div>

<figure id="mwBw" class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right"

srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/Bezier_basis.svg/500px-Bezier_basis.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x"
width="250" height="188" /></a>
<figcaption>The <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basis_function"
id="mwCw" rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Basis function">basis functions</a>
on the range <span id="mwDA" class="nowrap" about="#mwt2"
typeof="mw:Transclusion"
data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;nowrap&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Nowrap&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;&#39;&#39;t&#39;&#39; in [0,1]&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><em>t</em>
in [0,1]</span> for cubic Bézier curves: <span id="mwDQ" class="nowrap"
about="#mwt3" typeof="mw:Transclusion"
data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;nowrap&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Nowrap&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;blue: &#39;&#39;y&#39;&#39; {{=}} (1 − &#39;&#39;t&#39;&#39;)&lt;sup&gt;3&lt;/sup&gt;&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}">blue:
<em>y</em> = (1 − <em>t</em>)<sup>3</sup></span>, <span id="mwDg"
class="nowrap" about="#mwt4" typeof="mw:Transclusion"
data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;nowrap&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Nowrap&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;green: &#39;&#39;y&#39;&#39; {{=}} 3(1 − &#39;&#39;t&#39;&#39;)&lt;sup&gt;2&lt;/sup&gt;&#39;&#39;t&#39;&#39;&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}">green:
<em>y</em> = 3(1 − <em>t</em>)<sup>2</sup><em>t</em></span>, <span
id="mwDw" class="nowrap" about="#mwt5" typeof="mw:Transclusion"
data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;nowrap&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Nowrap&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;red: &#39;&#39;y&#39;&#39; {{=}} 3(1 − &#39;&#39;t&#39;&#39;)&#39;&#39;t&#39;&#39;&lt;sup&gt;2&lt;/sup&gt;&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}">red:
<em>y</em> = 3(1 − <em>t</em>)<em>t</em><sup>2</sup></span>, and <span
id="mwEA" class="nowrap" about="#mwt6" typeof="mw:Transclusion"
data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;nowrap&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Nowrap&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;cyan: &#39;&#39;y&#39;&#39; {{=}} &#39;&#39;t&#39;&#39;&lt;sup&gt;3&lt;/sup&gt;&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}">cyan:
<em>y</em> = <em>t</em><sup>3</sup></span>.</figcaption>
</figure>

A **Bézier curve** (<span id="mwEw" class="rt-commentedText nowrap"
about="#mwt7" typeof="mw:Transclusion"
mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;IPAc-en&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:IPAc-en&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;'&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;b&quot;},&quot;3&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;E&quot;},&quot;4&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;z&quot;},&quot;5&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;.&quot;},&quot;6&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;i&quot;},&quot;7&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;.&quot;},&quot;8&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;ei&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt"
lang="en-fonipa"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span
style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span
title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span
title="&#39;b&#39; in &#39;buy&#39;">b</span><span
title="/ɛ/: &#39;e&#39; in &#39;dress&#39;">ɛ</span><span
title="&#39;z&#39; in &#39;zoom&#39;">z</span><span
title="/./: syllable break">.</span><span
title="/i/: &#39;y&#39; in &#39;happy&#39;">i</span><span
title="/./: syllable break">.</span><span
title="/eɪ/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;face&#39;">eɪ</span></span>/</a></span></span>
<a
href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key"
id="mwFA" rel="mw:WikiLink" about="#mwt8" typeof="mw:Transclusion"
data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Respell&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Respell&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;BEH&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;zee&quot;},&quot;3&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;ay&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"
title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><em><span
style="font-size:90%">BEH</span>-zee-ay</em></a>,<sup><a href="#cite_note-1" id="mwFQ"><span id="mwFg"
class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwFw"
class="cite-bracket">[</span>1<span id="mwGA"
class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a></sup>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1177148991" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt9" id="mwGQ" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:IPA/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;IPA&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:IPA&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;fr&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;bezje&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}">.mw-parser-output .IPA-label-small{font-size:85%}.mw-parser-output .references .IPA-label-small,.mw-parser-output .infobox .IPA-label-small,.mw-parser-output .navbox .IPA-label-small{font-size:100%}</style><span class="IPA-label IPA-label-small"
about="#mwt9">French pronunciation:</span><span about="#mwt9">
</span><span class="IPA nowrap" lang="fr-Latn-fonipa"
about="#mwt9"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/French"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Help:IPA/French"><span
typeof="mw:Entity">[</span>bezje<span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></a></span>)
is a
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parametric_equation" id="mwGw"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Parametric equation">parametric curve</a> used
in <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer_graphics" id="mwHA"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Computer graphics">computer graphics</a> and
related
fields.<sup><a href="#cite_note-Mortenson-2" id="mwHQ"><span id="mwHg"
class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwHw"
class="cite-bracket">[</span>2<span id="mwIA"
class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a></sup> A
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sequence_(mathematics)" id="mwIQ"
class="mw-redirect" rel="mw:WikiLink"
title="Sequence (mathematics)">sequence</a> of discrete "control points"
defines a smooth, continuous curve by means of a formula. Usually the
curve is intended to approximate a real-world shape that otherwise has
no mathematical representation or whose representation is unknown or too
complicated. The Bézier curve is named after
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France" id="mwIg"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="France">French</a> engineer
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre_Bézier" id="mwIw"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Pierre Bézier">Pierre Bézier</a> (1910–1999),
who used it in the 1960s for designing curves for the bodywork of
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renault" id="mwJA"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Renault">Renault</a>
cars.<sup><a href="#cite_note-Hazewinkel-3" id="mwJQ"><span id="mwJg"
class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwJw"
class="cite-bracket">[</span>3<span id="mwKA"
class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a></sup> Other uses include the
design of computer
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Font" id="mwKQ" rel="mw:WikiLink"
title="Font">fonts</a> and
animation.<sup><a href="#cite_note-Hazewinkel-3" id="mwKg"><span id="mwKw"
class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwLA"
class="cite-bracket">[</span>3<span id="mwLQ"
class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a></sup> Bézier curves can be
combined to form a
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Composite_Bézier_curve" id="mwLg"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Composite Bézier curve">Bézier spline</a>, or
generalized to higher dimensions to form
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bézier_surface" id="mwLw"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Bézier surface">Bézier surfaces</a>.<sup><a href="#cite_note-Hazewinkel-3" id="mwMA"><span id="mwMQ"
class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwMg"
class="cite-bracket">[</span>3<span id="mwMw"
class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a></sup> The
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bézier_triangle" id="mwNA"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Bézier triangle">Bézier triangle</a> is a
special case of the latter.

In <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vector_graphics" id="mwNg"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Vector graphics">vector graphics</a>, Bézier
curves are used to model smooth curves that can be scaled indefinitely.
"Paths", as they are commonly referred to in image manipulation
programs,<sup><a href="#cite_note-4" id="mwNw" data-mw-group="note"><span id="mwOA"
class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwOQ"
class="cite-bracket">[</span>note 1<span id="mwOg"
class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a></sup> are combinations of
linked Bézier curves. Paths are not bound by the limits of
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raster_graphics" id="mwOw"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Raster graphics">rasterized</a> images and are
intuitive to modify.

Bézier curves are also used in the time domain, particularly in
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animation" id="mwPQ"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Animation">animation</a>,<sup><a href="#cite_note-:0-5" id="mwPg"><span id="mwPw"
class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwQA"
class="cite-bracket">[</span>4<span id="mwQQ"
class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a><a href="#cite_note-6" id="mwQg" data-mw-group="note"><span id="mwQw"
class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwRA"
class="cite-bracket">[</span>note 2<span id="mwRQ"
class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a></sup>
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/User_interface" id="mwRg"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="User interface">user interface</a> design and
smoothing cursor trajectory in eye gaze-controlled
interfaces.<sup><a href="#cite_note-7" id="mwRw"><span id="mwSA"
class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwSQ"
class="cite-bracket">[</span>5<span id="mwSg"
class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a></sup> For example, a Bézier
curve can be used to specify the velocity over time of an object such as
an icon moving from A to B, rather than simply moving at a fixed number
of pixels per step. When animators or
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graphical_user_interface"
id="mwSw" rel="mw:WikiLink"
title="Graphical user interface">interface</a> designers talk about the
"physics" or "feel" of an operation, they may be referring to the
particular Bézier curve used to control the velocity over time of the
move in question.

This also applies to robotics where the motion of a welding arm, for
example, should be smooth to avoid unnecessary wear.

</div>

<div id="mwTg" class="section" mw-section-id="1"
aria-labelledby="Invention">

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2">

## Invention

<span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\[</span>[edit](/w/index.php?title=B%C3%A9zier_curve&action=edit&section=1 "Edit section: Invention")<span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\]</span></span>

</div>

The mathematical basis for Bézier curves—the
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernstein_polynomial" id="mwUA"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Bernstein polynomial">Bernstein polynomials</a>—was
established in 1912, but the
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polynomial" id="mwUQ"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Polynomial">polynomials</a> were not applied to
graphics until some 50 years later when mathematician
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_de_Casteljau" id="mwUg"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Paul de Casteljau">Paul de Casteljau</a> in
1959 developed
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Casteljau&#39;s_algorithm"
id="mwUw" rel="mw:WikiLink" title="De Casteljau&#39;s algorithm">de
Casteljau's algorithm</a>, a
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Numerical_stability" id="mwVA"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Numerical stability">numerically stable</a>
method for evaluating the curves, and became the first to apply them to
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer-aided_design" id="mwVQ"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Computer-aided design">computer-aided
design</a> at French automaker
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citroën" id="mwVg"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Citroën">Citroën</a>.<sup><a href="#cite_note-FarinHoschek2002-8" id="mwVw"><span id="mwWA"
class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwWQ"
class="cite-bracket">[</span>6<span id="mwWg"
class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a></sup> De Casteljau's method was
patented in France but not published until the
1980s<sup><a href="#cite_note-deCasteljau1986-9" id="mwWw"><span id="mwXA"
class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwXQ"
class="cite-bracket">[</span>7<span id="mwXg"
class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a></sup> while the Bézier
polynomials were widely publicised in the 1960s by the
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France" id="mwXw"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="France">French</a> engineer
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre_Bézier" id="mwYA"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Pierre Bézier">Pierre Bézier</a>, who
discovered them independently and used them to design
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automobile" id="mwYQ"
class="mw-redirect" rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Automobile">automobile</a>
bodies at <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renault" id="mwYg"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Renault">Renault</a>.

</div>

<div id="mwYw" class="section" mw-section-id="2"
aria-labelledby="Specific_cases">

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2">

## Specific cases

<span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\[</span>[edit](/w/index.php?title=B%C3%A9zier_curve&action=edit&section=2 "Edit section: Specific cases")<span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\]</span></span>

</div>

A Bézier curve is defined by a sequence of
*<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Control_point_(mathematics)"
id="mwZg" rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Control point (mathematics)">control
points</a>* **P**<sub>0</sub> through **P**<sub>*n*</sub>, where *n* is
called the order of the curve (*n* = 1 for linear, 2 for quadratic, 3
for cubic, etc.). The first and last control points are always the
endpoints of the curve; however, the intermediate control points
generally do not lie on the curve. The sums in the following sections
are to be understood as <a
href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affine_space#Affine_combinations_and_barycenter"
id="mwbg" rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Affine space">affine combinations</a>
– that is, the coefficients sum to 1.

<div id="mwbw" class="section" mw-section-id="3"
aria-labelledby="Linear_Bézier_curves">

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3">

### <span id="Linear_B.C3.A9zier_curves" typeof="mw:FallbackId"></span>Linear Bézier curves

<span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\[</span>[edit](/w/index.php?title=B%C3%A9zier_curve&action=edit&section=3 "Edit section: Linear Bézier curves")<span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\]</span></span>

</div>

Given distinct points **P**<sub>0</sub> and **P**<sub>1</sub>, a linear
Bézier curve is simply a
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Straight_line" id="mwdQ"
class="mw-redirect" rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Straight line">line</a>
between those two points. The curve is given by

<span id="mweA" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt40"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{B}(t)=\\mathbf{P}_0 + t(\\mathbf{P}_1-\\mathbf{P}_0)=(1-t)\\mathbf{P}_0 + t\\mathbf{P}_1,\\ 0 \\le t \\le 1&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=\mathbf {P} _{0}+t(\mathbf {P} _{1}-\mathbf {P} _{0})=(1-t)\mathbf {P} _{0}+t\mathbf {P} _{1},\ 0\leq t\leq 1}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/b176a486b925ffb40cc6afddb2594982ca116e77"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:54.789ex; height:2.843ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=\mathbf {P} _{0}+t(\mathbf {P} _{1}-\mathbf {P} _{0})=(1-t)\mathbf {P} _{0}+t\mathbf {P} _{1},\ 0\leq t\leq 1}" /></span>

This is the simplest and is equivalent to
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linear_interpolation" id="mweg"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Linear interpolation">linear interpolation</a>.<sup><a href="#cite_note-10" id="mwew"><span id="mwfA"
class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwfQ"
class="cite-bracket">[</span>8<span id="mwfg"
class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a></sup> The quantity
<span id="mwfw" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt44"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{P}_1-\\mathbf{P}_0&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {P} _{1}-\mathbf {P} _{0}}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/c6ab77b94c18a0ef1c642da9289567198c367035"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -0.671ex; width:8.602ex; height:2.509ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {P} _{1}-\mathbf {P} _{0}}" /></span>
represents the
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Displacement_vector" id="mwgA"
class="mw-redirect" rel="mw:WikiLink"
title="Displacement vector">displacement vector</a> from the start point
to the end point.

</div>

<div id="mwgQ" class="section" mw-section-id="4"
aria-labelledby="Quadratic_Bézier_curves">

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3">

### <span id="Quadratic_B.C3.A9zier_curves" typeof="mw:FallbackId"></span>Quadratic Bézier curves

<span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\[</span>[edit](/w/index.php?title=B%C3%A9zier_curve&action=edit&section=4 "Edit section: Quadratic Bézier curves")<span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\]</span></span>

</div>

<figure id="mwgg" class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb">
<a
href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Quadratic_Beziers_in_string_art.svg"
id="mwgw" class="mw-file-description"><img
src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Quadratic_Beziers_in_string_art.svg/250px-Quadratic_Beziers_in_string_art.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail"
id="mwhA" class="mw-file-element mw-file-upright"
style="--mw-file-upright: 0.75"
resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Quadratic_Beziers_in_string_art.svg"
decoding="async" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="1024"
data-file-type="drawing"
srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Quadratic_Beziers_in_string_art.svg/500px-Quadratic_Beziers_in_string_art.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x"
width="190" height="380" /></a>
<figcaption>Quadratic Béziers in <a
href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/String_art" id="mwhg"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="String art">string art</a>: The end points
(<strong>•</strong>) and control point (<strong>×</strong>) define the
quadratic Bézier curve (<strong>⋯</strong>).</figcaption>
</figure>

A quadratic Bézier curve is the path traced by the
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Function_(mathematics)" id="mwiw"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Function (mathematics)">function</a>
**B**(*t*), given points **P**<sub>0</sub>, **P**<sub>1</sub>, and
**P**<sub>2</sub>,

<span id="mwlg" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt45"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{B}(t) = (1 - t)[(1 - t) \\mathbf P_0 + t \\mathbf P_1] + t [(1 - t) \\mathbf P_1 + t \\mathbf P_2],\\ 0 \\le t \\le 1&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=(1-t)[(1-t)\mathbf {P} _{0}+t\mathbf {P} _{1}]+t[(1-t)\mathbf {P} _{1}+t\mathbf {P} _{2}],\ 0\leq t\leq 1}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/9079c197914f4bb93341b43c37018543920684fd"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:63.731ex; height:2.843ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=(1-t)[(1-t)\mathbf {P} _{0}+t\mathbf {P} _{1}]+t[(1-t)\mathbf {P} _{1}+t\mathbf {P} _{2}],\ 0\leq t\leq 1}" /></span>,

which can be interpreted as the
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linear_interpolation" id="mwmA"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Linear interpolation">linear interpolant</a> of
corresponding points on the linear Bézier curves from **P**<sub>0</sub>
to **P**<sub>1</sub> and from **P**<sub>1</sub> to **P**<sub>2</sub>
respectively. Rearranging the preceding equation yields:

<span id="mwow" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt46"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{B}(t) = (1 - t)^{2}\\mathbf{P}_0 + 2(1 - t)t\\mathbf{P}_1 + t^{2}\\mathbf{P}_2,\\ 0 \\le t \\le 1.&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=(1-t)^{2}\mathbf {P} _{0}+2(1-t)t\mathbf {P} _{1}+t^{2}\mathbf {P} _{2},\ 0\leq t\leq 1.}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/a4626e1c50ffac3721ec7af506ebd7e3273c4068"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:51.849ex; height:3.176ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=(1-t)^{2}\mathbf {P} _{0}+2(1-t)t\mathbf {P} _{1}+t^{2}\mathbf {P} _{2},\ 0\leq t\leq 1.}" /></span>

This can be written in a way that highlights the symmetry with respect
to **P**<sub>1</sub>:

<span id="mwqQ" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt47"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{B}(t) = \\mathbf{P}_1+(1 - t)^{2}(\\mathbf{P}_0 - \\mathbf{P}_1) + t^{2}(\\mathbf{P}_2-\\mathbf{P}_1),\\ 0 \\le t \\le 1.&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=\mathbf {P} _{1}+(1-t)^{2}(\mathbf {P} _{0}-\mathbf {P} _{1})+t^{2}(\mathbf {P} _{2}-\mathbf {P} _{1}),\ 0\leq t\leq 1.}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/1f42abd9efcf2f4ee220b2bcad236c0216cabf21"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:58.256ex; height:3.176ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=\mathbf {P} _{1}+(1-t)^{2}(\mathbf {P} _{0}-\mathbf {P} _{1})+t^{2}(\mathbf {P} _{2}-\mathbf {P} _{1}),\ 0\leq t\leq 1.}" /></span>

Which immediately gives the
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derivative" id="mwqw"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Derivative">derivative</a> of the Bézier curve
with respect to *t*:

<span id="mwrw" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt48"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{B}'(t) = 2 (1 - t) (\\mathbf{P}_1 - \\mathbf{P}_0) + 2 t (\\mathbf{P}_2 - \\mathbf{P}_1),&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} '(t)=2(1-t)(\mathbf {P} _{1}-\mathbf {P} _{0})+2t(\mathbf {P} _{2}-\mathbf {P} _{1}),}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/0a93079a7237f00f26dc34243c09b40c9338e3b5"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:42.46ex; height:3.009ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} &#39;(t)=2(1-t)(\mathbf {P} _{1}-\mathbf {P} _{0})+2t(\mathbf {P} _{2}-\mathbf {P} _{1}),}" /></span>

from which it can be concluded that the
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tangent" id="mwsQ"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Tangent">tangents</a> to the curve at
**P**<sub>0</sub> and **P**<sub>2</sub> intersect at **P**<sub>1</sub>.
As *t* increases from 0 to 1, the curve departs from **P**<sub>0</sub>
in the direction of **P**<sub>1</sub>, then bends to arrive at
**P**<sub>2</sub> from the direction of **P**<sub>1</sub>.

The second derivative of the Bézier curve with respect to *t* is

<span id="mwxQ" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt49"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{B}''(t) = 2(\\mathbf{P}_2 - 2 \\mathbf{P}_1 + \\mathbf{P}_0).&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} ''(t)=2(\mathbf {P} _{2}-2\mathbf {P} _{1}+\mathbf {P} _{0}).}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/2fba252792cb84f6900f0f36ddba4f3631789d13"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:27.89ex; height:3.009ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} &#39;&#39;(t)=2(\mathbf {P} _{2}-2\mathbf {P} _{1}+\mathbf {P} _{0}).}" /></span>

</div>

<div id="mwxg" class="section" mw-section-id="5"
aria-labelledby="Cubic_Bézier_curves">

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3">

### <span id="Cubic_B.C3.A9zier_curves" typeof="mw:FallbackId"></span>Cubic Bézier curves

<span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\[</span>[edit](/w/index.php?title=B%C3%A9zier_curve&action=edit&section=5 "Edit section: Cubic Bézier curves")<span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\]</span></span>

</div>

Four points **P**<sub>0</sub>, **P**<sub>1</sub>, **P**<sub>2</sub> and
**P**<sub>3</sub> in the plane or in higher-dimensional space define a
cubic Bézier curve. The curve starts at **P**<sub>0</sub> going toward
**P**<sub>1</sub> and arrives at **P**<sub>3</sub> coming from the
direction of **P**<sub>2</sub>. Usually, it will not pass through
**P**<sub>1</sub> or **P**<sub>2</sub>; these points are only there to
provide directional information. The distance between **P**<sub>1</sub>
and **P**<sub>2</sub> determines "how far" and "how fast" the curve
moves towards **P**<sub>1</sub> before turning towards
**P**<sub>2</sub>.

Writing
**B**<sub>**P**<sub>*i*</sub>,**P**<sub>*j*</sub>,**P**<sub>*k*</sub></sub>(*t*)
for the quadratic Bézier curve defined by points **P**<sub>*i*</sub>,
**P**<sub>*j*</sub>, and **P**<sub>*k*</sub>, the cubic Bézier curve can
be defined as an affine combination of two quadratic Bézier curves:

<span id="mw_A" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt50"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{B}(t) = (1-t)\\mathbf{B}_{\\mathbf P_0,\\mathbf P_1,\\mathbf P_2}(t) + t \\mathbf{B}_{\\mathbf P_1,\\mathbf P_2,\\mathbf P_3}(t),\\ 0 \\le t \\le 1.&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=(1-t)\mathbf {B} _{\mathbf {P} _{0},\mathbf {P} _{1},\mathbf {P} _{2}}(t)+t\mathbf {B} _{\mathbf {P} _{1},\mathbf {P} _{2},\mathbf {P} _{3}}(t),\ 0\leq t\leq 1.}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/9ec9abff441b63fc458ee4e22a5ccd3449b767a7"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -1.005ex; width:53.737ex; height:3.009ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=(1-t)\mathbf {B} _{\mathbf {P} _{0},\mathbf {P} _{1},\mathbf {P} _{2}}(t)+t\mathbf {B} _{\mathbf {P} _{1},\mathbf {P} _{2},\mathbf {P} _{3}}(t),\ 0\leq t\leq 1.}" /></span>

The explicit form of the curve is:

<span id="mwAQA" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt51"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{B}(t) = (1-t)^3\\mathbf{P}_0+3(1-t)^2t\\mathbf{P}_1+3(1-t)t^2\\mathbf{P}_2+t^3\\mathbf{P}_3,\\ 0 \\le t \\le 1.&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=(1-t)^{3}\mathbf {P} _{0}+3(1-t)^{2}t\mathbf {P} _{1}+3(1-t)t^{2}\mathbf {P} _{2}+t^{3}\mathbf {P} _{3},\ 0\leq t\leq 1.}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/a4a840bf8e22c383e6841ca3cb01563b4632ad6f"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:68.332ex; height:3.176ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=(1-t)^{3}\mathbf {P} _{0}+3(1-t)^{2}t\mathbf {P} _{1}+3(1-t)t^{2}\mathbf {P} _{2}+t^{3}\mathbf {P} _{3},\ 0\leq t\leq 1.}" /></span>

For some choices of **P**<sub>1</sub> and **P**<sub>2</sub> the curve
may intersect itself or contain a
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cusp_(singularity)" id="mwAQY"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Cusp (singularity)">cusp</a>.

Any series of 4 distinct points can be converted to a cubic Bézier curve
that goes through all 4 points in order. Given the starting and ending
point of some cubic Bézier curve, and the points along the curve
corresponding to *t*<span id="mwAQk" typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>= 1/3
and *t*<span id="mwAQs" typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>=<span id="mwAQw"
typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>2/3, the control points for the original
Bézier curve can be
recovered.<sup><a href="#cite_note-11" id="mwAQ0"><span id="mwAQ4"
class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwAQ8"
class="cite-bracket">[</span>9<span id="mwARA"
class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a></sup>

The derivative of the cubic Bézier curve with respect to *t* is

<span id="mwARU" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt55"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{B}'(t) = 3(1-t)^2(\\mathbf{P}_1 - \\mathbf{P}_0) + 6(1-t)t(\\mathbf{P}_2 - \\mathbf{P}_1) + 3t^2(\\mathbf{P}_3 - \\mathbf{P}_2) \\,.&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} '(t)=3(1-t)^{2}(\mathbf {P} _{1}-\mathbf {P} _{0})+6(1-t)t(\mathbf {P} _{2}-\mathbf {P} _{1})+3t^{2}(\mathbf {P} _{3}-\mathbf {P} _{2})\,.}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/bda9197c2e77c17d90839b951cb0035d79c8d417"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:66.861ex; height:3.176ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} &#39;(t)=3(1-t)^{2}(\mathbf {P} _{1}-\mathbf {P} _{0})+6(1-t)t(\mathbf {P} _{2}-\mathbf {P} _{1})+3t^{2}(\mathbf {P} _{3}-\mathbf {P} _{2})\,.}" /></span>

The second derivative of the Bézier curve with respect to *t* is

<span id="mwARo" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt56"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{B}''(t) = 6(1-t)(\\mathbf{P}_2 - 2 \\mathbf{P}_1 + \\mathbf{P}_0) + 6t(\\mathbf{P}_3 - 2 \\mathbf{P}_2 + \\mathbf{P}_1) \\,.&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} ''(t)=6(1-t)(\mathbf {P} _{2}-2\mathbf {P} _{1}+\mathbf {P} _{0})+6t(\mathbf {P} _{3}-2\mathbf {P} _{2}+\mathbf {P} _{1})\,.}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/fb11bad4e0889aa759ccd6f746e636d3b996b7cd"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:57.067ex; height:3.009ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} &#39;&#39;(t)=6(1-t)(\mathbf {P} _{2}-2\mathbf {P} _{1}+\mathbf {P} _{0})+6t(\mathbf {P} _{3}-2\mathbf {P} _{2}+\mathbf {P} _{1})\,.}" /></span>

<figure id="mwARs" class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb">
<a
href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Tschirnhausen_cubic_spline.svg"
id="mwARw" class="mw-file-description"><img
src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Tschirnhausen_cubic_spline.svg/250px-Tschirnhausen_cubic_spline.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail"
id="mwAR0" class="mw-file-element"
resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Tschirnhausen_cubic_spline.svg"
decoding="async" data-file-width="399" data-file-height="252"
data-file-type="drawing"
srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Tschirnhausen_cubic_spline.svg/500px-Tschirnhausen_cubic_spline.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x"
width="250" height="158" /></a>
<figcaption>A cubic Bézier curve (red) whose control path (dotted black
lines) forms two congruent <a
href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isosceles_triangle" id="mwAR8"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Isosceles triangle">isosceles triangles</a>
(blue) takes the form of an arc of the <a
href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tschirnhausen_cubic" id="mwASA"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Tschirnhausen cubic">Tschirnhausen
cubic</a>.</figcaption>
</figure>

For control points whose consecutive triples form two
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Similar_triangles" id="mwASI"
class="mw-redirect" rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Similar triangles">similar
triangles</a> with the same orientation, the resulting cubic Bézier
curve is an arc of a (scaled and translated)
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tschirnhausen_cubic" id="mwASM"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Tschirnhausen cubic">Tschirnhausen cubic</a>.
These are
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pythagorean_hodograph_curve"
id="mwASQ" rel="mw:WikiLink"
title="Pythagorean hodograph curve">Pythagorean hodograph curves</a>,
curves whose
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arc_length" id="mwASU"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Arc length">arc length</a> and
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Offset_curve" id="mwASY"
class="mw-redirect" rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Offset curve">offset
curves</a> have a rational parameterization, and they are the only cubic
curves with these
properties.<sup><a href="#cite_note-12" id="mwASc"><span id="mwASg"
class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwASk"
class="cite-bracket">[</span>10<span id="mwASo"
class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a></sup>

</div>

</div>

<div id="mwASs" class="section" mw-section-id="6"
aria-labelledby="General_definition">

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2">

## General definition

<span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\[</span>[edit](/w/index.php?title=B%C3%A9zier_curve&action=edit&section=6 "Edit section: General definition")<span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\]</span></span>

</div>

Bézier curves can be defined for any degree *n*.

<div id="mwAS4" class="section" mw-section-id="7"
aria-labelledby="Recursive_definition">

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3">

### Recursive definition

<span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\[</span>[edit](/w/index.php?title=B%C3%A9zier_curve&action=edit&section=7 "Edit section: Recursive definition")<span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\]</span></span>

</div>

A
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recursive_definition" id="mwATA"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Recursive definition">recursive definition</a>
for the Bézier curve of degree *n* expresses it as a point-to-point
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linear_combination" id="mwATI"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Linear combination">linear combination</a>
(<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linear_interpolation" id="mwATM"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Linear interpolation">linear interpolation</a>)
of a pair of corresponding points in two Bézier curves of degree
*n*<span id="mwATU" typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>−<span id="mwATY"
typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>1.

Let <span id="mwATg" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt60"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{B}_{\\mathbf{P}_0\\mathbf{P}_1\\ldots\\mathbf{P}_k}&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} _{\mathbf {P} _{0}\mathbf {P} _{1}\ldots \mathbf {P} _{k}}}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/e7ab52ac2f4f863095636beda1e20a1d6fad5254"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -1.005ex; width:10.457ex; height:2.843ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} _{\mathbf {P} _{0}\mathbf {P} _{1}\ldots \mathbf {P} _{k}}}" /></span>
denote the Bézier curve determined by any selection of points
**P**<sub>0</sub>, **P**<sub>1</sub>,<span id="mwAT0"
typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>...,<span id="mwAT4"
typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>**P**<sub>*k*</sub>. Then to start,

<span id="mwAUQ" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt61"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{B}_{\\mathbf{P}_0}(t) = \\mathbf{P}_0 \\text{, and}&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} _{\mathbf {P} _{0}}(t)=\mathbf {P} _{0}{\text{, and}}}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/2bca12603d35eb2ecf31aeb12b526491a0df8ab4"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -1.005ex; width:17.86ex; height:3.009ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} _{\mathbf {P} _{0}}(t)=\mathbf {P} _{0}{\text{, and}}}" /></span>

<span id="mwAUc" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt62"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{B}(t) = \\mathbf{B}_{\\mathbf{P}_0\\mathbf{P}_1\\ldots\\mathbf{P}_n}(t) = (1-t)\\mathbf{B}_{\\mathbf{P}_0\\mathbf{P}_1\\ldots\\mathbf{P}_{n-1}}(t) + t\\mathbf{B}_{\\mathbf{P}_1\\mathbf{P}_2\\ldots\\mathbf{P}_n}(t)&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=\mathbf {B} _{\mathbf {P} _{0}\mathbf {P} _{1}\ldots \mathbf {P} _{n}}(t)=(1-t)\mathbf {B} _{\mathbf {P} _{0}\mathbf {P} _{1}\ldots \mathbf {P} _{n-1}}(t)+t\mathbf {B} _{\mathbf {P} _{1}\mathbf {P} _{2}\ldots \mathbf {P} _{n}}(t)}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/3e330568539637d3c302301b2a8b5a06a9ec5cad"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -1.005ex; width:62.417ex; height:3.009ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=\mathbf {B} _{\mathbf {P} _{0}\mathbf {P} _{1}\ldots \mathbf {P} _{n}}(t)=(1-t)\mathbf {B} _{\mathbf {P} _{0}\mathbf {P} _{1}\ldots \mathbf {P} _{n-1}}(t)+t\mathbf {B} _{\mathbf {P} _{1}\mathbf {P} _{2}\ldots \mathbf {P} _{n}}(t)}" /></span>

This recursion is elucidated in the
<a href="#Constructing_Bézier_curves" id="mwAUk"
class="mw-selflink-fragment" rel="mw:WikiLink">animations below</a>.

</div>

<div id="mwAUo" class="section" mw-section-id="8"
aria-labelledby="Explicit_definition">

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3">

### Explicit definition

<span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\[</span>[edit](/w/index.php?title=B%C3%A9zier_curve&action=edit&section=8 "Edit section: Explicit definition")<span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\]</span></span>

</div>

The formula can be expressed explicitly as follows (where t<sup>0</sup>
and (1-t)<sup>0</sup> are extended continuously to be 1 throughout
\[0,1\]):

<span id="mwAVA" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt63"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\begin{align}\n\\mathbf{B}(t) &amp;= \\sum_{i=0}^n {n\\choose i}(1 - t)^{n - i}t^i\\mathbf{P}_i \\\\\n              &amp;=(1 - t)^n\\mathbf{P}_0 + {n\\choose 1}(1 - t)^{n - 1}t\\mathbf{P}_1 + \\cdots + {n\\choose n - 1}(1 - t)t^{n - 1}\\mathbf{P}_{n - 1} + t^n\\mathbf{P}_n, &amp;&amp; 0 \\leqslant t \\leqslant 1\n\\end{align}&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle {\begin{aligned}\mathbf {B} (t)&=\sum _{i=0}^{n}{n \choose i}(1-t)^{n-i}t^{i}\mathbf {P} _{i}\\&=(1-t)^{n}\mathbf {P} _{0}+{n \choose 1}(1-t)^{n-1}t\mathbf {P} _{1}+\cdots +{n \choose n-1}(1-t)t^{n-1}\mathbf {P} _{n-1}+t^{n}\mathbf {P} _{n},&&0\leqslant t\leqslant 1\end{aligned}}}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/4aead49f2dc6a80c8f4018274355e8f48c38573a"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -6.005ex; width:96.275ex; height:13.176ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle {\begin{aligned}\mathbf {B} (t)&amp;=\sum _{i=0}^{n}{n \choose i}(1-t)^{n-i}t^{i}\mathbf {P} _{i}\\&amp;=(1-t)^{n}\mathbf {P} _{0}+{n \choose 1}(1-t)^{n-1}t\mathbf {P} _{1}+\cdots +{n \choose n-1}(1-t)t^{n-1}\mathbf {P} _{n-1}+t^{n}\mathbf {P} _{n},&amp;&amp;0\leqslant t\leqslant 1\end{aligned}}}" /></span>

where <span id="mwAVI" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt64"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\scriptstyle {n \\choose i}&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \scriptstyle {n \choose i}}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/87b707252a528faf2acafab1eae4d06e2850b517"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -1.338ex; width:2.763ex; height:3.509ex;"
aria-hidden="true" alt="{\displaystyle \scriptstyle {n \choose i}}" /></span>
are the
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Binomial_coefficient" id="mwAVM"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Binomial coefficient">binomial coefficients</a>.

For example, when *n*<span id="mwAVY"
typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>=<span id="mwAVc"
typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>5:

<span id="mwAVo" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt65"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\begin{align}\n\\mathbf{B}(t) &amp;= (1 - t)^5\\mathbf{P}_0 + 5t(1 - t)^4\\mathbf{P}_1 + 10t^2(1 - t)^3 \\mathbf{P}_2 + 10t^3 (1-t)^2 \\mathbf{P}_3 + 5t^4(1-t) \\mathbf{P}_4 + t^5 \\mathbf{P}_5, &amp;&amp; 0 \\leqslant t \\leqslant 1.\n\\end{align}&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle {\begin{aligned}\mathbf {B} (t)&=(1-t)^{5}\mathbf {P} _{0}+5t(1-t)^{4}\mathbf {P} _{1}+10t^{2}(1-t)^{3}\mathbf {P} _{2}+10t^{3}(1-t)^{2}\mathbf {P} _{3}+5t^{4}(1-t)\mathbf {P} _{4}+t^{5}\mathbf {P} _{5},&&0\leqslant t\leqslant 1.\end{aligned}}}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/3ddc46eb1462ba204537bdc8b17926c28cfd096e"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -1.005ex; width:108.053ex; height:3.176ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle {\begin{aligned}\mathbf {B} (t)&amp;=(1-t)^{5}\mathbf {P} _{0}+5t(1-t)^{4}\mathbf {P} _{1}+10t^{2}(1-t)^{3}\mathbf {P} _{2}+10t^{3}(1-t)^{2}\mathbf {P} _{3}+5t^{4}(1-t)\mathbf {P} _{4}+t^{5}\mathbf {P} _{5},&amp;&amp;0\leqslant t\leqslant 1.\end{aligned}}}" /></span>

</div>

<div id="mwAVs" class="section" mw-section-id="9"
aria-labelledby="Terminology">

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3">

### Terminology

<span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\[</span>[edit](/w/index.php?title=B%C3%A9zier_curve&action=edit&section=9 "Edit section: Terminology")<span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\]</span></span>

</div>

Some terminology is associated with these parametric curves. We have

<span id="mwAV8" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt66"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{B}(t) = \\sum_{i=0}^n b_{i, n}(t)\\mathbf{P}_i,\\ \\ \\ 0 \\le t \\le 1&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=\sum _{i=0}^{n}b_{i,n}(t)\mathbf {P} _{i},\ \ \ 0\leq t\leq 1}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/488a961c0e8d062d66528e757be4b3de33d071c0"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -3.005ex; width:32.044ex; height:6.843ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=\sum _{i=0}^{n}b_{i,n}(t)\mathbf {P} _{i},\ \ \ 0\leq t\leq 1}" /></span>

where the polynomials

<span id="mwAWM" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt67"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;b_{i,n}(t) = {n \\choose i} t^i (1 - t)^{n - i},\\ \\ \\ i = 0, \\ldots, n&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle b_{i,n}(t)={n \choose i}t^{i}(1-t)^{n-i},\ \ \ i=0,\ldots ,n}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/7cef695905a81857bb4bb618e55f17d1150db7f8"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -2.505ex; width:39.572ex; height:6.176ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle b_{i,n}(t)={n \choose i}t^{i}(1-t)^{n-i},\ \ \ i=0,\ldots ,n}" /></span>

are known as
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernstein_polynomial" id="mwAWU"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Bernstein polynomial">Bernstein basis
polynomials</a> of degree *n*.

*t*<sup>0</sup><span id="mwAWo"
typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>=<span id="mwAWs"
typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>1, (1<span id="mwAWw"
typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>−<span id="mwAW0"
typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>*t*)<sup>0</sup><span id="mwAXA"
typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>=<span id="mwAXE"
typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>1, and the
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Binomial_coefficient" id="mwAXI"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Binomial coefficient">binomial coefficient</a>,
<span id="mwAXM" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt68"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\scriptstyle {n \\choose i}&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \scriptstyle {n \choose i}}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/87b707252a528faf2acafab1eae4d06e2850b517"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -1.338ex; width:2.763ex; height:3.509ex;"
aria-hidden="true" alt="{\displaystyle \scriptstyle {n \choose i}}" /></span>,
is:

<span id="mwAXY" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt69"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;{n \\choose i} = \\frac{n!}{i!(n - i)!}.&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle {n \choose i}={\frac {n!}{i!(n-i)!}}.}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/e17fe109e65b8606880e4eafb4fe9620c7a837c3"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -2.671ex; width:18.34ex; height:6.343ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle {n \choose i}={\frac {n!}{i!(n-i)!}}.}" /></span>

The points **P**<sub>*i*</sub> are called *control points* for the
Bézier curve. The
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polygon" id="mwAXw"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Polygon">polygon</a> formed by connecting the
Bézier points with
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_(mathematics)" id="mwAX0"
class="mw-redirect" rel="mw:WikiLink"
title="Line (mathematics)">lines</a>, starting with **P**<sub>0</sub>
and finishing with **P**<sub>*n*</sub>, is called the *Bézier polygon*
(or *control polygon*). The
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convex_hull" id="mwAYU"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Convex hull">convex hull</a> of the Bézier
polygon contains the Bézier curve.

</div>

<div id="mwAYY" class="section" mw-section-id="10"
aria-labelledby="Polynomial_form">

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3">

### Polynomial form

<span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\[</span>[edit](/w/index.php?title=B%C3%A9zier_curve&action=edit&section=10 "Edit section: Polynomial form")<span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\]</span></span>

</div>

Sometimes it is desirable to express the Bézier curve as a
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polynomial" id="mwAYg"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Polynomial">polynomial</a> instead of a sum of
less straightforward
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernstein_polynomial" id="mwAYk"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Bernstein polynomial">Bernstein polynomials</a>.
Application of the
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Binomial_theorem" id="mwAYo"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Binomial theorem">binomial theorem</a> to the
definition of the curve followed by some rearrangement will yield

<span id="mwAY0" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt70"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{B}(t) = \\sum_{j = 0}^n {t^j \\mathbf{C}_j}&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=\sum _{j=0}^{n}{t^{j}\mathbf {C} _{j}}}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/1263b2329c8a60a78a433731dfd88b55d6a37eb0"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -3.338ex; width:15.981ex; height:7.176ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)=\sum _{j=0}^{n}{t^{j}\mathbf {C} _{j}}}" /></span>

where

<span id="mwAZE" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt71"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{C}_j = \\frac{n!}{(n - j)!} \\sum_{i = 0}^j \\frac{(-1)^{i + j} \\mathbf{P}_i}{i! (j - i)!} =\n\\prod_{m = 0}^{j - 1} (n - m) \\sum_{i = 0}^j \\frac{(-1)^{i + j} \\mathbf{P}_i}{i! (j - i)!}.&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {C} _{j}={\frac {n!}{(n-j)!}}\sum _{i=0}^{j}{\frac {(-1)^{i+j}\mathbf {P} _{i}}{i!(j-i)!}}=\prod _{m=0}^{j-1}(n-m)\sum _{i=0}^{j}{\frac {(-1)^{i+j}\mathbf {P} _{i}}{i!(j-i)!}}.}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/4ec4133b96de55e83a63096d58b53945f15520d7"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -3.005ex; width:60.053ex; height:7.343ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {C} _{j}={\frac {n!}{(n-j)!}}\sum _{i=0}^{j}{\frac {(-1)^{i+j}\mathbf {P} _{i}}{i!(j-i)!}}=\prod _{m=0}^{j-1}(n-m)\sum _{i=0}^{j}{\frac {(-1)^{i+j}\mathbf {P} _{i}}{i!(j-i)!}}.}" /></span>

This could be practical if <span id="mwAZM"
class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt72"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{C}_j&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {C} _{j}}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/041ea30ddcc44f9b1f33e4d3bc4e1bb3c359a98c"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -1.005ex; width:2.841ex; height:2.843ex;"
aria-hidden="true" alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {C} _{j}}" /></span> can
be computed prior to many evaluations of <span id="mwAZQ"
class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt73"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{B}(t)&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/fd11dd93082b513feb5fadd44385f91fa7d59bac"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:4.55ex; height:2.843ex;"
aria-hidden="true" alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} (t)}" /></span>;
however one should use caution as high order curves may lack
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Numeric_stability" id="mwAZU"
class="mw-redirect" rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Numeric stability">numeric
stability</a>
(<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Casteljau&#39;s_algorithm"
id="mwAZY" rel="mw:WikiLink" title="De Casteljau&#39;s algorithm">de
Casteljau's algorithm</a> should be used if this occurs). Note that the
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empty_product" id="mwAZc"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Empty product">empty product</a> is 1.

</div>

<div id="mwAZg" class="section" mw-section-id="11"
aria-labelledby="Properties">

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3">

### Properties

<span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\[</span>[edit](/w/index.php?title=B%C3%A9zier_curve&action=edit&section=11 "Edit section: Properties")<span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\]</span></span>

</div>

<figure id="mwAZk" class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb">
<a
href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Quadratic_to_cubic_Bezier_curve.svg"
id="mwAZo" class="mw-file-description"><img
src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Quadratic_to_cubic_Bezier_curve.svg/250px-Quadratic_to_cubic_Bezier_curve.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail"
id="mwAZs" class="mw-file-element mw-file-upright"
style="--mw-file-upright: 0.75"
resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Quadratic_to_cubic_Bezier_curve.svg"
decoding="async" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="512"
data-file-type="drawing"
srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Quadratic_to_cubic_Bezier_curve.svg/500px-Quadratic_to_cubic_Bezier_curve.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x"
width="190" height="190" /></a>
<figcaption>A cubic Bézier curve (yellow) can be made identical to a
quadratic one (black) by
<div id="mwAZ0" style="margin-left:1em;text-indent:-1em">
1. copying the end points, and
</div>
<div id="mwAZ4" style="margin-left:1em;text-indent:-1em">
2. placing its two middle control points (yellow circles) two thirds
along the line segments from the end points to the quadratic curve's
middle control point (black rectangle).
</div></figcaption>
</figure>

- <span id="mwAaA">The curve begins at <span id="mwAaE"
  class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
  typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt74"
  mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf P_0&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
  style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {P} _{0}}`$</span><img
  src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/de9d076fbce9ad4a62c1e769a98d9f0720a295ad"
  class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
  style="vertical-align: -0.671ex; width:2.881ex; height:2.509ex;"
  aria-hidden="true" alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {P} _{0}}" /></span>
  and ends at <span id="mwAaI"
  class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
  typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt75"
  mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf P_n&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
  style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {P} _{n}}`$</span><img
  src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/be7379cde6612ac7bc018f83c272ac36b3341211"
  class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
  style="vertical-align: -0.671ex; width:3.045ex; height:2.509ex;"
  aria-hidden="true" alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {P} _{n}}" /></span>;
  this is the so-called *endpoint interpolation* property.</span>
- <span id="mwAaQ">The curve is a line
  <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/If_and_only_if" id="mwAaU"
  rel="mw:WikiLink" title="If and only if">if and only if</a> all the
  control points are
  <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collinear" id="mwAaY"
  class="mw-redirect" rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Collinear">collinear</a>.</span>
- <span id="mwAac">The start and end of the curve is
  <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tangent" id="mwAag"
  rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Tangent">tangent</a> to the first and last
  section of the Bézier polygon, respectively.</span>
- <span id="mwAak">A curve can be split at any point into two subcurves,
  or into arbitrarily many subcurves, each of which is also a Bézier
  curve.</span>
- <span id="mwAao">Some curves that seem simple, such as the
  <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circle" id="mwAas"
  rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Circle">circle</a>, cannot be described
  exactly by a Bézier or
  <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piecewise" id="mwAaw"
  class="mw-redirect" rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Piecewise">piecewise</a>
  Bézier curve; though a four-piece cubic Bézier curve can approximate a
  circle (see
  <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Composite_Bézier_curve"
  id="mwAa0" rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Composite Bézier curve">composite
  Bézier curve</a>), with a maximum radial error of less than one part
  in a thousand, when each inner control point (or offline point) is the
  distance <span id="mwAa4"
  class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
  typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt76"
  mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\textstyle\\frac{4\\left(\\sqrt {2}-1\\right)}{3}&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
  style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \textstyle {\frac {4\left({\sqrt {2}}-1\right)}{3}}}`$</span><img
  src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/c9cb318f36b8f02dabaf2331385c454e13087bd9"
  class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
  style="vertical-align: -1.338ex; width:7.455ex; height:4.676ex;"
  aria-hidden="true"
  alt="{\displaystyle \textstyle {\frac {4\left({\sqrt {2}}-1\right)}{3}}}" /></span>
  horizontally or vertically from an outer control point on a
  <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unit_circle" id="mwAa8"
  rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Unit circle">unit circle</a>. More generally,
  an *n*-piece cubic Bézier curve can approximate a circle, when each
  inner control point is the distance <span id="mwAbE"
  class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
  typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt77"
  mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\textstyle\\frac{4}{3}\\tan(t/4)&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
  style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \textstyle {\frac {4}{3}}\tan(t/4)}`$</span><img
  src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/f7d45ab3e22bffce62847c2be211630de2c82cb9"
  class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
  style="vertical-align: -1.338ex; width:10.379ex; height:3.676ex;"
  aria-hidden="true"
  alt="{\displaystyle \textstyle {\frac {4}{3}}\tan(t/4)}" /></span>
  from an outer control point on a unit circle, where <span id="mwAbI"
  class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
  typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt78"
  mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;display&quot;:&quot;inline&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;t = 2\\pi/n&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
  style="display: none;">$`{\textstyle t=2\pi /n}`$</span><img
  src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/229b11dea07621ef29b016dcf318babe1016ad83"
  class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
  style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:8.99ex; height:2.843ex;"
  aria-hidden="true" alt="{\textstyle t=2\pi /n}" /></span> (i.e.
  <span id="mwAbM" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
  typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt79"
  mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;t=360^\\circ/n&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
  style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle t=360^{\circ }/n}`$</span><img
  src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/daec9dc3c3edb4d825e319a5e84effccd3367e03"
  class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
  style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:11.037ex; height:2.843ex;"
  aria-hidden="true" alt="{\displaystyle t=360^{\circ }/n}" /></span>),
  and <span id="mwAbQ" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
  typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt80"
  mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;n&gt;2&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
  style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle n>2}`$</span><img
  src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/44e71ac55b9fbf1e9f341b946cda63d61d3ef2cd"
  class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
  style="vertical-align: -0.338ex; width:5.656ex; height:2.176ex;"
  aria-hidden="true" alt="{\displaystyle n&gt;2}" /></span>.</span>
- <span id="mwAbU">Every quadratic Bézier curve is also a cubic Bézier
  curve, and more generally, every degree *n* Bézier curve is also a
  degree *m* curve for any *m* \> *n*. In detail, a degree *n* curve
  with control points <span id="mwAbs"
  class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
  typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt81"
  mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{P}_0,\\,\\dots,\\,\\mathbf{P}_n&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
  style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {P} _{0},\,\dots ,\,\mathbf {P} _{n}}`$</span><img
  src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/866a6f90083b5e35d08d95eef98e54acc89b9320"
  class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
  style="vertical-align: -0.671ex; width:11.879ex; height:2.509ex;"
  aria-hidden="true"
  alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {P} _{0},\,\dots ,\,\mathbf {P} _{n}}" /></span>
  is equivalent (including the parametrization) to the degree *n* + 1
  curve with control points <span id="mwAb0"
  class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
  typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt82"
  mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{P}'_0,\\,\\dots,\\,\\mathbf {P}'_{n+1}&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
  style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {P} '_{0},\,\dots ,\,\mathbf {P} '_{n+1}}`$</span><img
  src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/2b672af82bf08ac3acd0c64114a4f6bc38fc42e5"
  class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
  style="vertical-align: -1.005ex; width:13.979ex; height:3.176ex;"
  aria-hidden="true"
  alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {P} &#39;_{0},\,\dots ,\,\mathbf {P} &#39;_{n+1}}" /></span>,
  where <span id="mwAb4"
  class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
  typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt83"
  mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf P'_k=\\tfrac{k}{n+1}\\mathbf P_{k-1}+\\left(1-\\tfrac{k}{n+1}\\right)\\mathbf P_k&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
  style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {P} '_{k}={\tfrac {k}{n+1}}\mathbf {P} _{k-1}+\left(1-{\tfrac {k}{n+1}}\right)\mathbf {P} _{k}}`$</span><img
  src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/02108c05594d1f070a72d184131c816c86434f46"
  class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
  style="vertical-align: -1.838ex; width:31.797ex; height:4.843ex;"
  aria-hidden="true"
  alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {P} &#39;_{k}={\tfrac {k}{n+1}}\mathbf {P} _{k-1}+\left(1-{\tfrac {k}{n+1}}\right)\mathbf {P} _{k}}" /></span>,
  <span id="mwAb8" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
  typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt84"
  mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\forall k = 0,\\,1,\\,\\dots,\\,n,\\,n+1&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
  style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \forall k=0,\,1,\,\dots ,\,n,\,n+1}`$</span><img
  src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/d2f6f65d739294f0f1cf38cfa0d05bf2e82036eb"
  class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
  style="vertical-align: -0.671ex; width:23.514ex; height:2.509ex;"
  aria-hidden="true"
  alt="{\displaystyle \forall k=0,\,1,\,\dots ,\,n,\,n+1}" /></span> and
  define <span id="mwAcA"
  class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
  typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt85"
  mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{P}_{n+1} := \\mathbf{P}_{0}&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
  style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {P} _{n+1}:=\mathbf {P} _{0}}`$</span><img
  src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/b37edc19331d2195bcf06fb7c9c2f44a7f724a97"
  class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
  style="vertical-align: -0.671ex; width:11.772ex; height:2.509ex;"
  aria-hidden="true"
  alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {P} _{n+1}:=\mathbf {P} _{0}}" /></span>,
  <span id="mwAcE" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
  typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt86"
  mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{P}_{-1} := \\mathbf{P}_{n}&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
  style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {P} _{-1}:=\mathbf {P} _{n}}`$</span><img
  src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/b75ffbdd58c9b8e790a7748ed50f945d4db63c77"
  class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
  style="vertical-align: -0.671ex; width:10.95ex; height:2.509ex;"
  aria-hidden="true"
  alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {P} _{-1}:=\mathbf {P} _{n}}" /></span>.</span>
- <span id="mwAcI">Bézier curves have the
  <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Variation_diminishing_property"
  id="mwAcM" rel="mw:WikiLink"
  title="Variation diminishing property">variation diminishing
  property</a>. What this means in intuitive terms is that a Bézier
  curve does not "undulate" more than the polygon of its control points,
  and may actually "undulate" less than
  that.<sup><a href="#cite_note-GonzalezDiaz-Herrera2014-13" id="mwAcQ"><span
  id="mwAcU" class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwAcY"
  class="cite-bracket">[</span>11<span id="mwAcc"
  class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a></sup></span>
- <span id="mwAcg">There is no <a
  href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_control?action=edit&amp;redlink=1"
  id="mwAck" class="new" rel="mw:WikiLink" typeof="mw:LocalizedAttrs"
  data-mw-i18n="{&quot;title&quot;:{&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;x-page&quot;,&quot;key&quot;:&quot;red-link-title&quot;,&quot;params&quot;:[&quot;Local control&quot;]}}"
  title="Local control (page does not exist)">local control</a> in
  degree *n* Bézier curves—meaning that any change to a control point
  requires recalculation of and thus affects the aspect of the entire
  curve, "although the further that one is from the control point that
  was changed, the smaller is the change in the
  curve".<sup><a href="#cite_note-Agoston2005-14" id="mwAcs"><span id="mwAcw"
  class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwAc0"
  class="cite-bracket">[</span>12<span id="mwAc4"
  class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a></sup></span>
- <span id="mwAc8">A Bézier curve of order higher than two may intersect
  itself or have a
  <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cusp_(singularity)" id="mwAdA"
  rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Cusp (singularity)">cusp</a> for certain
  choices of the control points.</span>

</div>

<div id="mwAdE" class="section" mw-section-id="12"
aria-labelledby="Second-order_curve_is_a_parabolic_segment">

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3">

### Second-order curve is a parabolic segment

<span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\[</span>[edit](/w/index.php?title=B%C3%A9zier_curve&action=edit&section=12 "Edit section: Second-order curve is a parabolic segment")<span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\]</span></span>

</div>

<figure id="mwAdI" class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb">
<a
href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Quadratic_Bezier_parabola_equivalence.svg"
id="mwAdM" class="mw-file-description"><img
src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Quadratic_Bezier_parabola_equivalence.svg/250px-Quadratic_Bezier_parabola_equivalence.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail"
id="mwAdQ" class="mw-file-element mw-file-upright"
style="--mw-file-upright: 0.75"
resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Quadratic_Bezier_parabola_equivalence.svg"
decoding="async" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="768"
data-file-type="drawing"
srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Quadratic_Bezier_parabola_equivalence.svg/500px-Quadratic_Bezier_parabola_equivalence.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x"
width="190" height="285" /></a>
<figcaption>Equivalence of a quadratic Bézier curve and a parabolic
segment</figcaption>
</figure>

A quadratic Bézier curve is also a segment of a
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parabola" id="mwAdc"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Parabola">parabola</a>. As a parabola is a
<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conic_section" id="mwAdg"
rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Conic section">conic section</a>, some sources
refer to quadratic Béziers as "conic
arcs".<sup><a href="#cite_note-freetype-15" id="mwAdk"><span id="mwAdo"
class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwAds"
class="cite-bracket">[</span>13<span id="mwAdw"
class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a></sup> With reference to the
figure on the right, the important features of the parabola can be
derived as
follows:<sup><a href="#cite_note-16" id="mwAd0"><span id="mwAd4"
class="mw-reflink-text"><span id="mwAd8"
class="cite-bracket">[</span>14<span id="mwAeA"
class="cite-bracket">]</span></span></a></sup>

1.  <span id="mwAeI">Tangents to the parabola at the endpoints of the
    curve (A and B) intersect at its control point (C).</span>
2.  <span id="mwAeM">If D is the midpoint of AB, the tangent to the
    curve which is
    <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perpendicular" id="mwAeQ"
    rel="mw:WikiLink" title="Perpendicular">perpendicular</a> to CD
    (dashed cyan line) defines its vertex (V). Its axis of symmetry
    (dash-dot cyan) passes through V and is perpendicular to the
    tangent.</span>
3.  <span id="mwAeU">E is either point on the curve with a tangent at
    45° to CD (dashed green). If G is the intersection of this tangent
    and the axis, the line passing through G and perpendicular to CD is
    the directrix (solid green).</span>
4.  <span id="mwAeY">The focus (F) is at the intersection of the axis
    and a line passing through E and perpendicular to CD (dotted
    yellow). The latus rectum is the line segment within the curve
    (solid yellow).</span>

</div>

<div id="mwAec" class="section" mw-section-id="13"
aria-labelledby="Derivative">

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3">

### Derivative

<span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\[</span>[edit](/w/index.php?title=B%C3%A9zier_curve&action=edit&section=13 "Edit section: Derivative")<span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\]</span></span>

</div>

The derivative for a curve of order *n* is

<span id="mwAew" class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline"
typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt97"
mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;\\mathbf{B}'(t) = n \\sum_{i=0}^{n-1} b_{i,n-1}(t) (\\mathbf{P}_{i+1} - \\mathbf{P}_i).&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y"
style="display: none;">$`{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} '(t)=n\sum _{i=0}^{n-1}b_{i,n-1}(t)(\mathbf {P} _{i+1}-\mathbf {P} _{i}).}`$</span><img
src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/4a79736c89090b4a3ee83d5572d4631cac5f881e"
class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert"
style="vertical-align: -3.005ex; width:34.497ex; height:7.343ex;"
aria-hidden="true"
alt="{\displaystyle \mathbf {B} &#39;(t)=n\sum _{i=0}^{n-1}b_{i,n-1}(t)(\mathbf {P} _{i+1}-\mathbf {P} _{i}).}" /></span>

</div>

</div>

<div id="mwAe0" class="section" mw-section-id="14"
aria-labelledby="Constructing_Bézier_curves">

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2">

## <span id="Constructing_B.C3.A9zier_curves" typeof="mw:FallbackId"></span>Constructing Bézier curves

<span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\[</span>[edit](/w/index.php?title=B%C3%A9zier_curve&action=edit&section=14 "Edit section: Constructing Bézier curves")<span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\]</span></span>

</div>

<div id="mwAe4" class="section" mw-section-id="15"
aria-labelledby="Linear_curves">

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3">

### <span id="constructing-bezier-curves"></span>Linear curves

<span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\[</span>[edit](/w/index.php?title=B%C3%A9zier_curve&action=edit&section=15 "Edit section: Linear curves")<span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\]</span></span>

</div>

Let *t* denote the fraction of progress (from 0 to 1) the point
**B**(*t*) has made along its traversal from **P**<sub>0</sub> to
**P**<sub>1</sub>. For example, when *t*=0.25, **B**(*t*) is one quarter
of the way from point **P**<sub>0</sub> to **P**<sub>1</sub>. As *t*
varies from 0 to 1, **B**(*t*) draws a line from **P**<sub>0</sub> to
**P**<sub>1</sub>.

|  |
|----|
| <span id="mwAgo" typeof="mw:File" mw="{&quot;caption&quot;:&quot;Animation of a linear Bézier curve, &lt;i id=\&quot;mwAgk\&quot;&gt;t&lt;/i&gt; in [0,1]&quot;}"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bézier_1_big.gif" id="mwAgs"
class="mw-file-description"
title="Animation of a linear Bézier curve, t in [0,1]"><img
src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/00/B%C3%A9zier_1_big.gif/250px-B%C3%A9zier_1_big.gif?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail"
id="mwAgw" class="mw-file-element"
resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bézier_1_big.gif"
decoding="async" data-file-width="360" data-file-height="150"
data-file-type="bitmap"
srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/0/00/B%C3%A9zier_1_big.gif?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail_unscaled 2x"
width="240" height="100"
alt="Animation of a linear Bézier curve, t in [0,1]" /></a></span> |
| Animation of a linear Bézier curve, *t* in \[0,1\] |

</div>

<div id="mwAhA" class="section" mw-section-id="16"
aria-labelledby="Quadratic_curves">

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3">

### Quadratic curves

<span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\[</span>[edit](/w/index.php?title=B%C3%A9zier_curve&action=edit&section=16 "Edit section: Quadratic curves")<span class="mw-editsection-bracket">\]</span></span>

</div>

For quadratic Bézier curves one can construct intermediate points
**Q**<sub>0</sub> and **Q**<sub>1</sub> such that as *t* varies from 0
to 1:

- <span id="mwAhg">Point **Q**<sub>0</sub>(*t*) varies from
  **P**<sub>0</sub> to **P**<sub>1</sub> and describes a linear Bézier
  curve.</span>
- <span id="mwAiA">Point **Q**<sub>1</sub>(*t*) varies from
  **P**<sub>1</sub> to **P**<sub>2</sub> and describes a linear Bézier
  curve.</span>
- <span id="mwAig">Point **B**(*t*) is interpolated linearly between
  **Q**<sub>0</sub>(*t*) to **Q**<sub>1</sub>(*t*) and describes a
  quadratic Bézier curve.</span>

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
| <span id="mwAjU" typeof="mw:File" mw="{&quot;caption&quot;:&quot;Construction of a quadratic Bézier curve&quot;}"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bézier_2_big.svg" id="mwAjY"
class="mw-file-description"
title="Construction of a quadratic Bézier curve"><img
src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/B%C3%A9zier_2_big.svg/250px-B%C3%A9zier_2_big.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail"
id="mwAjc" class="mw-file-element"

decoding="async" data-file-width="360" data-file-height="150"

width="240" height="100"
alt="Construction of a quadratic Bézier curve" /></a></span> |  | <span id="mwAjs" typeof="mw:File" mw="{&quot;caption&quot;:&quot;Animation of a quadratic Bézier curve, &lt;i id=\&quot;mwAjo\&quot;&gt;t&lt;/i&gt; in [0,1]&quot;}"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bézier_2_big.gif" id="mwAjw"
class="mw-file-description"

width="240" height="100"
alt="Animation of a quadratic Bézier curve, t in [0,1]" /></a></span> |
